height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=144.2615pt\hbox{\kern 3.06668pt$\hyperlink{rule:tm-wait}{\textsc{\small[tm-wait]}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 28.2511pt\hbox{$\displaystyle y:{?}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}end}\vdash^{0}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}wait}\,y.\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}done}$}}}}\enskip\penalty$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=247.60701pt\hbox{\kern 3.06668pt$\hyperlink{rule:tm-wait}{\textsc{\small[tm-wait]}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 69.6172pt\hbox{$\displaystyle x:{?}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}end},y:{?}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}end}\vdash^{0}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}wait}\,x\dots$}}}}\enskip\penalty$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=350.95251pt\hbox{\kern 3.06668pt$\hyperlink{rule:tm-tag}{\textsc{\small[tm-tag]}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 102.69269pt\hbox{$\displaystyle x:\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}c}{?}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}box}.{?}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}end},y:{?}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}end}\vdash^{0}x[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}c}]\mathsf{?}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}box}\dots$}}}}\enskip\penalty$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=444.20592pt\hbox{\kern 3.06668pt$\hyperlink{rule:tm-tag}{\textsc{\small[tm-tag]}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 143.40268pt\hbox{$\displaystyle x:\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}s}{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}ok}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}c}{?}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}box}.{?}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}end},y:{?}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}end}\vdash^{0}x[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}s}]\mathsf{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}ok}\dots$}}}}\enskip\penalty$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=537.45932pt\hbox{\kern 3.06668pt$\hyperlink{rule:tm-cast}{\textsc{\small[tm-cast]}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 224.89758pt\hbox{$\displaystyle x:S,y:{?}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}end}\vdash^{1}\lceil x\rceil\dots$}}}}\enskip\penalty\vdots

[tm-tag]

\displaystyle x:S,y:\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}b}_{2}{?}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}yes}.{?}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}end}+\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}b}_{2}{?}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}no}.T\vdash^{1}y[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}b}_{2}]\mathsf{?}\{\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}yes}\dots,\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}no}\dots\}

Note how the application of
[tm-cast]
is key to change the type of
x
in
the branch where the proposed split is accepted by
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}b}_{2}
. In that branch,

x
is deterministically used to send an
ok
message and we leverage on the
fair subtyping relation
S\leqslant_{1}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}s}{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}ok}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}c}{?}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}box}.{?}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}end}
.

For the branch in which
\textit{Buyer}_{1}
sends
giveup
we obtain the following
derivation tree:

[tm-done]

\displaystyle\emptyset\vdash^{0}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}done}

[tm-wait]

\displaystyle x:{?}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}end}\vdash^{0}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}wait}\,x.\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}done}

\displaystyle x:\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}s}{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}cancel}.{?}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}end}\vdash^{0}x[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}s}]\mathsf{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}cancel}.\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}wait}\,x.\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}done}

[tm-cast]

\displaystyle x:S\vdash^{1}\lceil x\rceil x[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}s}]\mathsf{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}cancel}.\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}wait}\,x.\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}done}

[tm-wait]

\displaystyle x:S,y:{?}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}end}\vdash^{1}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}wait}\,y.\lceil x\rceil x[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}s}]\mathsf{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}cancel}.\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}wait}\,x.\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}done}

Once again the cast is necessary to change the type of
x
, but this time
leveraging on the fair subtyping relation
S\leqslant_{1}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}s}{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}cancel}.{?}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}end}
.
These two derivations can then be combined to complete the proof that the body
of
\textit{Buyer}_{1}
is well typed:

\displaystyle\penalty\qquad\mathstrut\smash{\vdots}\qquad\qquad\qquad\smash{\vdots}\qquad

[tm-tag]

\displaystyle x:S,y:T\vdash^{1}y[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}b}_{2}]\mathsf{!}\{\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}split}\dots,\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}giveup}\dots\}

Clearly, it is also necessary to find finite derivation trees for all of the
judgments shown above. This can be easily achieved using the corule

[com-tag]
.

\lrcorner

Example 5.2.3
.

Casts can be useful to reconcile the types of a channel that is used
differently in different branches of a non-deterministic choice. For
example, below is an alternative modeling of
Buyer
from
Example

5.1.1

where we abbreviate
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}seller}}
to
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}s}
for convenience:

B(x)\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\vartriangle}}{{=}}}}\lceil x\rceil x[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}s}]\mathsf{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}add}.x[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}s}]\mathsf{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}add}.B\langle x\rangle\oplus\lceil x\rceil x[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}s}]\mathsf{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}pay}.\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}close}\,x

Note that
x
is used for sending two
add
messages in the left branch of
the non-deterministic choice and for sending a single
pay
message in the
right branch. Given the session type
S=\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}s}{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}add}.S+\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}s}{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}pay}.{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}end}
and using the fair subtyping relations
S\leqslant_{2}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}s}{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}add}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}s}{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}add}.S
and
S\leqslant_{1}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}s}{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}pay}.{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}end}
we can obtain the following typing derivation
for the body of
B
(we show only the left branch as the right one contains a
straightforward application of
S\leqslant_{1}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}s}{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}pay}.{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}end}
):

\displaystyle\penalty\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 27.8558pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\mathstrut\smash{\vdots}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=55.71161pt\hbox{\kern 3.06668pt$\hyperlink{rule:tm-call}{\textsc{\small[tm-call]}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle x:S\vdash^{1}B\langle x\rangle$}}}}\enskip\penalty$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=151.94191pt\hbox{\kern 3.06668pt$\hyperlink{rule:tm-tag}{\textsc{\small[tm-tag]}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 17.67413pt\hbox{$\displaystyle x:\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}s}{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}add}.S\vdash^{1}x[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}s}]\mathsf{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}add}.B\langle x\rangle$}}}}\enskip\penalty$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=245.19531pt\hbox{\kern 3.06668pt$\hyperlink{rule:tm-tag}{\textsc{\small[tm-tag]}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 33.30429pt\hbox{$\displaystyle x:\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}s}{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}add}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}s}{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}add}.S\vdash^{1}x[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}s}]\mathsf{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}add}.x[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}s}]\mathsf{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}add}.B\langle x\rangle$}}}}\enskip\penalty$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=338.44872pt\hbox{\kern 3.06668pt$\hyperlink{rule:tm-cast}{\textsc{\small[tm-cast]}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 96.60118pt\hbox{$\displaystyle x:S\vdash^{3}\lceil x\rceil x[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}s}]\mathsf{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}add}.x[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}s}]\mathsf{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}add}.B\langle x\rangle$}}}}\enskip\penalty\vdots

[tm-choice]

\displaystyle x:S\vdash^{1}\lceil x\rceil x[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}s}]\mathsf{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}add}.x[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}s}]\mathsf{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}add}.B\langle x\rangle\oplus\lceil x\rceil x[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}s}]\mathsf{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}pay}.\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}close}\,x

In general, the transformation
u[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]\mathsf{!}\{\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}m}_{i}.P_{i}\}_{i=1..n}\leadsto\lceil u\rceil u[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]\mathsf{!}{\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}m}_{1}}.P_{1}\oplus\cdots\oplus\lceil u\rceil u[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]\mathsf{!}{\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}m}_{n}}.P_{n}
does not always preserve typing, so
it is not always possible to encode the output of tags using casts and
non-deterministic choices. As an example, the definition

\mathit{Slot}(x)\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\vartriangle}}{{=}}}}x[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]\mathsf{?}\{\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}play}.x[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]\mathsf{!}\{\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}win}.\mathit{Slot}\langle x\rangle,\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}lose}.\mathit{Slot}\langle x\rangle\},\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}quit}.\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}close}\,x\}

implements the unbiased slot machine of
Example

3.2.2
that waits
for a message indicating whether a
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}
wants to
play
another game or
to
quit
(we assign role
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}
to the player).
In the former case, the slot machine notifies
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}
of the
outcome (either
win
or
lose
).
It is easy to see that
\mathit{Slot}
is well typed under the global type
assignment
\mathit{Slot}:[T;0]
where
T=\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}{?}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}play}.(\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}win}.T+\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}lose}.T)+\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}{?}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}quit}.{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}end}
. In particular,
\mathit{Slot}
has rank
0

since it performs no casts and it creates no sessions. If we encode the tag
output in
\mathit{Slot}
using casts and non-deterministic choices we end up
with the following process definition, which is ill typed because it cannot
be given a finite rank:

\mathit{Slot}(x)\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\vartriangle}}{{=}}}}x[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]\mathsf{?}\{\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}play}.(\lceil x\rceil x[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]\mathsf{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}win}.\mathit{Slot}\langle x\rangle\oplus\lceil x\rceil x[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]\mathsf{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}lose}.\mathit{Slot}\langle x\rangle),\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}quit}.\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}close}\,x\}

The difference between this version of
\mathit{Slot}
and the above
definition of
B
is that
\mathit{Slot}
always recurs after a cast, so it
is not obvious that finitely many casts suffice in order for
\mathit{Slot}

to terminate.

\lrcorner

Example 5.2.4
.

Example

5.2.2

shows that casts are essential in the type derivation.
However, the process would be well typed if we considered a subtyping relation that does not preserve coherence

[
Gay and Hole 2005
]
for the involved types are finite.
Now we refine the buyer from
Example

5.1.1
in order to consider more involved sessions. Again, we
collapse role names to their initials.

\displaystyle B(x)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\vartriangle}}{{=}}}}{\lceil x\rceil B_{1}\langle x\rangle\oplus\lceil x\rceil B_{2}\langle x\rangle}

\displaystyle B_{1}(x)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\vartriangle}}{{=}}}}{x[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}s}]\mathsf{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}add}.x[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}s}]\mathsf{!}\{\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}add}.B_{1}\langle x\rangle,\,\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}pay}.\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}wait}\,x.{\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}done}}\}}

\displaystyle B_{2}(x)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\vartriangle}}{{=}}}}{x[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}s}]\mathsf{!}\{\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}add}.x[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}s}]\mathsf{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}add}.B_{2}\langle x\rangle,\,\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}pay}.\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}wait}\,x.{\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}done}}\}}

B_{2}
corresponds to the buyer in
Example

5.1.1
while
B_{1}
is the acquirer that adds an odd number of items to the cart.

B
non deterministically chooses to behave according to
B_{1}
or
B_{2}
.
Let
S_{b_{1}}
and
S_{b_{2}}
be the session types such that

x:S_{b_{1}}
in
B_{1}
and
x:S_{b_{2}}
in
B_{2}
respectively:

\begin{array}[]{ll}S_{b_{1}}=\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}s}{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}add}.(\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}s}{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}add}.S_{b_{1}}+\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}s}{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}pay}.{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}end})&S_{b_{2}}=\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}s}{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}add}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}s}{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}add}.S_{b_{2}}+\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}s}{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}pay}.{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}end}\end{array}

In
example

3.2.1
we showed that
S\leqslant_{1}S_{b_{2}}
where
S=\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}s}{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}add}.S+\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}s}{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}pay}.{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}end}

models the acquirer that adds arbitrarily many items to the cart. Analogously, we can prove that
S\leqslant_{2}S_{b_{1}}
.
Hence we derive

\displaystyle\penalty\vdots

[tm-call]

\displaystyle x:S_{b_{1}}\vdash^{0}B_{1}\langle x\rangle

[tm-cast]

\displaystyle x:S\vdash^{2}\lceil x\rceil B_{1}\langle x\rangle

\displaystyle\penalty\vdots

[tm-call]

\displaystyle x:S_{b_{2}}\vdash^{0}B_{2}\langle x\rangle

[tm-cast]

\displaystyle x:S\vdash^{1}\lceil x\rceil B_{2}\langle x\rangle

[tm-choice]

\displaystyle x:S\vdash^{1}\lceil x\rceil B_{1}\langle x\rangle\oplus\lceil x\rceil B_{2}\langle x\rangle

Again, the casts are crucial to obtain the type derivation of process
B
because rule

[tm-choice]
requires that
B_{1}
and
B_{2}
are typed in the same context.
Note that
B_{1}
and
B_{2}
are typed with rank 0 since no sessions are created and no casts are performed by the processes.

Example 5.2.5
.

Here we provide evidence that the process definitions in
Example

5.1.3
are
well typed, even if they model processes that can open arbitrarily many
sessions. In that example, the most interesting process definition is that
of the worker
Sort
, which is recursive and may create a new session. In
contrast,
Merge
is finite and
Main
only refers to
Sort
. We claim
that these process definitions are well typed under the global type
assignments

\textit{Main}:[();1]\qquad\textit{Sort}:[U;0]\qquad\textit{Merge}:[T,V;0]

where

\begin{array}[]{lll}T=\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}m}{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}res}.{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}end}&U=\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}m}{?}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}req}.T&V=\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}w}_{1}{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}req}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}w}_{2}{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}req}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}w}_{1}{?}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}res}.\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}w}_{2}{?}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}res}.{?}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}end}\end{array}

For the branch of
Sort
that creates a new session we obtain the
derivation tree

\displaystyle\penalty\mathstrut\smash{\vdots}

[tm-call]

\displaystyle x:T,t[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}m}]:V\vdash^{0}\textit{Merge}\langle x,t[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}m}]\rangle

\displaystyle\penalty\smash{\vdots}

\displaystyle t[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}w}_{i}]:U\vdash^{0}\textit{Sort}\langle t[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}w}_{i}]\rangle

[tm-par]

\displaystyle x:T\vdash^{1}(t)(\textit{Merge}\langle x,t[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}m}]\rangle\mathbin{|}\textit{Sort}\langle t[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}w}_{1}]\rangle\mathbin{|}\textit{Sort}\langle t[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}w}_{2}]\rangle)

where
i=1,2
. The rank
1
derives from the fact that the created session involves
three zero-ranked participants.
For the body of
Sort
we obtain the following derivation tree:

\displaystyle\penalty\smash{\vdots}

[tm-par]

\displaystyle x:T\vdash^{1}(t)(\textit{Merge}\langle x,t[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}m}]\rangle\mathbin{|}\cdots)

[tm-close]

\displaystyle x:{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}end}\vdash^{0}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}close}\,x

[tm-tag]

\displaystyle x:T\vdash^{0}x[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}m}]\mathsf{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}res}\dots

[tm-choice]

\displaystyle x:T\vdash^{0}(t)(\textit{Merge}\langle x,t[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}m}]\rangle\mathbin{|}\cdots)\oplus x[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}m}]\mathsf{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}res}\dots

[tm-tag]

\displaystyle x:U\vdash^{0}x[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}m}]\mathsf{?}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}req}.((t)(\textit{Merge}\langle x,t[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}m}]\rangle\mathbin{|}\cdots)\oplus x[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}m}]\mathsf{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}res}\dots)

In the application of the rule
[tm-choice]
, the rank of the whole
choice coincides with that of the branch in which no new sessions are
created. This way we account for the fact that, even though
Sort

may
create a new session, it does not
have to
do so in order
to terminate.

\lrcorner

5.3
Correctness

In this section we discuss the proof of
Theorem

5.2.1
.
The proof technique follows that of
Theorem

4.2.1
.
Such proof is essentially composed of a standard subject
reduction result showing that typing is preserved by reductions and a proof that
every well-typed process other than
done
may always reduce in such a way
that a suitably defined
well-founded measure
strictly decreases. The
measure is a lexicographically ordered pair of natural numbers with the
following meaning: the first component measures the number of sessions that must
be created and the total weight of casts that must be performed in order for the
process to terminate (this information is essentially the rank we associate with
typing judgments); the second component measures the overall effort required to
terminate every session that has already been created (these sessions are
identified by the fact that their restriction occurs unguarded in the process).
We account for this effort by measuring the shortest reduction that terminates a
coherent session map (
Definition

2.3.2
). The reason why we need two
quantities in the measure is that in general every application of fair subtyping
may
increase
the length of the shortest reduction that terminates a
coherent session map. So, when casts are performed the second component of the
measure may increase, but the first component reduces.
As a final remark, it should be noted that the overall measure associated with a
well-typed process
may also increase
, for example if new sessions are
created (
Example

5.1.3
). However, one particular reduction that decreases the
measure is always guaranteed to exist.
For the sake of clarity, in the following we often write
M~\text{coherent}
instead of
{\#}M
.

5.3.1
Subject Reduction

We show the proof of a standard subject reduction theorem (see
Lemma

5.3.3
). For this sake,
we need an additional result, that we dub subject congruence,
stating that well-typedness is preserved by the structural precongruence
relation for processes in
Figure

5.2
(
Lemma

5.3.2
).
Notably,
Lemma

5.3.2
tells that the rank does not incrase.
Such result is not provable in
Lemma

5.3.3
as the non deterministic choice
can reduce to a branch that increases the rank.

Lemma 5.3.1
.

If
\Gamma,x:S\vdash^{n}P
and
\Gamma,u:S
is defined, then
\Gamma,u:S\vdash^{n}P\{u/x\}
.
A typing context is
defined
if the endpoints occurring in it all have different session names.

Proof.

By bounded coinduction (see
Proposition

1.2.3
).
∎

Lemma 5.3.2

(Subject Congruence)
.

If
\Gamma\vdash^{n}P
and
P\preccurlyeq Q
, then
\Gamma\vdash^{m}Q
for some
m\leq n
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation of
P\preccurlyeq Q
and by cases on the last rule applied.

Case
[sm-par-comm]
.
Then
P=(s)(\overline{P}\mathbin{\mid}P^{\prime}\mathbin{\mid}Q^{\prime}\mathbin{\mid}\overline{Q})\preccurlyeq(s)(\overline{P}\mathbin{\mid}Q^{\prime}\mathbin{\mid}P^{\prime}\mathbin{\mid}\overline{Q})=Q
.
From rule
[tm-par]
we deduce that there exist
\Gamma_{i},\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i},S_{i},n_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,h
such that

•

\Gamma=\Gamma_{1},\dots,\Gamma_{h}

•

n=1+\sum_{i=1}^{h}n_{i}

•

\prod_{i=1}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}~\text{coherent}

•

\Gamma_{i},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}]:S_{i}\vdash^{n_{i}}P_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,k

•

\Gamma_{k+1},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{k+1}]:S_{k+1}\vdash^{n_{k+1}}P^{\prime}

•

\Gamma_{k+2},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{k+2}]:S_{k+2}\vdash^{n_{k+2}}Q^{\prime}

•

\Gamma_{i},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}]:S_{i}\vdash^{n_{i}}Q_{i}
for
i=k+3,\dots,h

We conclude
\Gamma\vdash^{m}Q
with one application of
[tm-par]
by taking
m\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny\sf def}}}{{=}}n
.

Case
[sm-par-assoc]
.
Then
P=(s)(\overline{P}\mathbin{\mid}(t)(R\mathbin{\mid}\overline{Q}))\preccurlyeq(t)((s)(\overline{P}\mathbin{\mid}R)\mathbin{\mid}\overline{Q})=Q
and
s\in\mathsf{fn}(R)
.
From rule
[tm-par]
we deduce that there exist
\Gamma_{i},\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i},S_{i},n_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,h
such that

•

\Gamma=\Gamma_{1},\dots,\Gamma_{h}

•

n=1+\sum_{i=1}^{h}n_{i}

•

\prod_{i=1}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}~\text{coherent}

•

\Gamma_{i},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}]:S_{i}\vdash^{n_{i}}P_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,h-1

•

\Gamma_{h},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{h}]:S_{h}\vdash^{n_{h}}(t)(R\mathbin{\mid}\overline{Q})

From rule
[tm-par]
and the hypothesis that
s\in\mathsf{fn}(R)
we deduce that there exist
\Delta_{i},\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}_{i},T_{i},m_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,k
such that

•

\Gamma_{h}=\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{k}

•

n_{h}=1+\sum_{1}^{k}m_{i}

•

\prod_{1}^{k}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}_{i}\triangleright{T_{i}}~\text{coherent}

•

\Delta_{1},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{h}]:S_{h},t[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}_{1}]:T_{1}\vdash^{m_{1}}R

•

\Delta_{i+1},t[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}_{i+1}]:T_{i+1}\vdash^{m_{i+1}}Q_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,k-1

Using
[tm-par]
we deduce

\Gamma_{1},\dots,\Gamma_{h-1},\Delta_{1},t[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}_{1}]:T_{1}\vdash^{1+\sum_{i=1}^{h-1}{n_{i}}+m_{1}}(s)(\overline{P}\mathbin{\mid}R)
.
We conclude
\Gamma\vdash^{m}(t)((s)(\overline{P}\mathbin{\mid}R)\mathbin{\mid}\overline{Q})
with another application of
[tm-par]
by taking
m\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny\sf def}}}{{=}}n
.

Case
[sm-cast-comm]
.
Then
P=\lceil u\rceil{\lceil v\rceil{R}}\preccurlyeq\lceil v\rceil{\lceil u\rceil{R}}=Q
. We can assume
u\neq v
or else
P=Q
.
From rule
[tm-cast]
we deduce that there exist
\Gamma_{1},S,T,n_{1},n_{u}
such that

•

\Gamma=\Gamma_{1},u:S

•

S\leqslant_{n_{u}}T

•

n=n_{u}+n_{1}

•

\Gamma_{1},u:T\vdash^{n_{1}}\lceil v\rceil{R}

From rule
[tm-cast]
we deduce that there exist
\Gamma_{2},S^{\prime},T^{\prime},n_{2},n_{v}
such that

•

\Gamma_{1}=\Gamma_{2},v:S^{\prime}

•

S^{\prime}\leqslant_{n_{v}}T^{\prime}

•

n_{1}=n_{v}+n_{2}

•

\Gamma_{2},u:T,v:T^{\prime}\vdash^{n_{2}}R

We derive
\Gamma_{2},u:S,v:T^{\prime}\vdash^{n_{u}+n_{2}}\lceil u\rceil{R}
with one application of
[tm-cast]

and we conclude with another application of
[tm-cast]
by taking
m\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny\sf def}}}{{=}}n
.

Case
[sm-cast-new]
.
Then
P=(s)(\lceil s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]\rceil{R}\mathbin{\mid}\overline{P})\preccurlyeq(s)(R\mathbin{\mid}\overline{P})=Q
.
From rule
[tm-par]
we deduce that there exist
\Delta,n^{\prime}
and
\Gamma_{i},\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}_{i},n_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,h
such that

•

\Gamma=\Delta,\Gamma_{1},\dots,\Gamma_{h}
for
i=1,\dots,h

•

n=1+n^{\prime}+\sum_{i=1}^{h}n_{i}

•

\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}\triangleright{S}\mathbin{\mid}\prod_{i=1}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}~\text{coherent}

•

\Delta,s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]:S\vdash^{n^{\prime}}\lceil s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]\rceil{R}

•

\Gamma_{i},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}_{i}]:S_{i}\vdash^{n_{i}}P_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,h

From rule
[tm-cast]
we deduce that there exist
T,m^{\prime},m_{s}
such that

•

S\leqslant_{m_{s}}T

•

n^{\prime}=m_{s}+m^{\prime}

•

\Delta,s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]:T\vdash^{m^{\prime}}R

From
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}\triangleright{S}\mathbin{\mid}\prod_{i=1}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}~\text{coherent}
,
S\leqslant_{m_{s}}T
and
definition

3.2.1
we deduce

\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}\triangleright{T}\mathbin{\mid}\prod_{i=1}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}~\text{coherent}
.
We conclude with an application of
[tm-par]
by taking
m\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny\sf def}}}{{=}}1+m^{\prime}+\sum_{i=1}^{h}n_{i}\leq n
.

Case
[sm-cast-swap]
.
Then
P=(s)(\lceil t[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]\rceil{R}\mathbin{\mid}\overline{P})\preccurlyeq\lceil t[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]\rceil{(s)(R\mathbin{\mid}\overline{P})}=Q
and
t\neq s
.
From rule
[tm-par]
we deduce that there exist
\Gamma_{i},\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}_{i},n_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,h
such that

•

\Gamma=\Gamma_{1},\dots,\Gamma_{h}
for
i=1,\dots,h

•

n=1+\sum_{i=1}^{h}n_{i}

•

\prod_{i=1}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}~\text{coherent}

•

\Gamma_{1},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}_{1}]:S_{1}\vdash^{n_{1}}\lceil t[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]\rceil{R}

•

\Gamma_{i},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}_{i}]:S_{i}\vdash^{n_{i}}P_{i}
for
i=2,\dots,h

From rule
[tm-cast]
we deduce that there exist
\Delta,T,n^{\prime},m_{t}
such that

•

\Gamma_{1}=\Delta,t[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]:S

•

S\leqslant_{m_{t}}T

•

n_{1}=m_{t}+n^{\prime}

•

\Delta,t[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]:T,s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}_{1}]:S_{1}\vdash^{n^{\prime}}R

We derive
\Delta,t[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]:T,\Gamma_{2},\dots,\Gamma_{h}\vdash^{1+n^{\prime}+\sum_{i=2}^{h}n_{i}}(s)(R\mathbin{\mid}\overline{P})

with an application of
[tm-par]
. We conclude with an application of
[tm-cast]
by taking
m\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny\sf def}}}{{=}}n
.

Case
[sm-call]
.
Then
P=A\langle\overline{u}\rangle\preccurlyeq R\{\overline{u}/\overline{x}\}=Q
and

A(\overline{x})\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\vartriangle}}{{=}}}}R
.
From
[tm-call]
we conclude that there exist
\overline{S}
and
m
such that

A:[\overline{S};m]
and
\Gamma=\overline{u:S}
and
\overline{u:S}\vdash^{m}Q
and
m\leq n
.
∎

Lemma 5.3.3

(Subject Reduction)
.

If
\Gamma\vdash^{n}P
and
P\to Q
, then
\Gamma\vdash^{m}Q
for some
m
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation of
P\to Q
and by cases on the last rule applied.

Case
[rm-choice]
.
Then
P=P_{1}\oplus P_{2}\to P_{k}=Q
and
k\in\{1,2\}
.
From
[tm-choice]
we deduce that
\Gamma\vdash^{m}Q
for some
m
.

Case
[rm-signal]
.
Then
P=(s)(\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}wait}\,s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}].{Q}\mathbin{|}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}close}\,s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}_{1}]\mathbin{|}\cdots\mathbin{|}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}close}\,s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}_{h}])\to Q

[tm-par]
,
[tm-wait]
and
[tm-close]
we deduce that there exist

such that

•

n=1+m+\sum_{i=1}^{h}n_{i}

•

\Gamma,s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]:{?}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}end}\vdash^{m}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}wait}\,s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}].{Q}

•

\Gamma\vdash^{m}Q

•

s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}_{i}]:{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}end}\vdash^{n_{i}}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}close}\,s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}_{i}]
for

i=1,\dots,h

There is nothing left to prove.

Case
[rm-channel]
.
Then
P=(s)(s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}][\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}]\mathsf{!}v.P^{\prime}\mathbin{|}s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}][\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]\mathsf{?}(x).Q^{\prime}\mathbin{|}\overline{R})\to(s)(P^{\prime}\mathbin{|}Q^{\prime}\{v/x\}\mathbin{|}\overline{R})=Q
.
From
[tm-par]
we deduce that there exist
\Gamma_{i},S_{i},\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i},n_{i}
for

•

n=1+\sum_{i=1}^{h}n_{i}

\prod_{i=1}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}~\text{coherent}

•

\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}=\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{1}
and
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}=\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{2}

•

\Gamma_{1},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]:S_{1}\vdash^{n_{1}}s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}][\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}]\mathsf{!}v.P^{\prime}

•

\Gamma_{2},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}]:S_{2}\vdash^{n_{2}}s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}][\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]\mathsf{?}(x).Q^{\prime}

•

\Gamma_{i},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}]:S_{i}\vdash^{n_{i}}R_{i}
for
i=3,\dots,h

From
[tm-channel-out]
and
[tm-channel-in]
we deduce that there exist
S_{v},T_{1},T_{2},\Delta_{1}
such that

•

S_{1}=\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}{!}{S_{v}}.T_{1}

•

\Gamma_{1}=\Delta_{1},v:S_{v}

•

\Delta_{1},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]:T_{1}\vdash^{n_{1}}P^{\prime}

•

S_{2}=\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}{?}{S_{v}}.T_{2}

•

\Gamma_{2},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}]:T_{2},x:S_{v}\vdash^{n_{2}}Q^{\prime}

Using
lemma

5.3.1
we deduce
\Gamma_{2},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}]:T_{2},v:S_{v}\vdash^{n_{2}}Q^{\prime}\{v/x\}
. Using
definition

2.3.2
we deduce
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}\triangleright{T_{1}}\mathbin{|}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}\triangleright{T_{2}}\mathbin{|}\prod_{i=3}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}~\text{coherent}
. We conclude with
one application of
[tm-par]
taking
m\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny\sf def}}}{{=}}n
.

Case
[rm-pick]
.
Then
P=(s)(s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}][\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}]\mathsf{!}\{\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}m}_{i}.P_{i}\}_{i\in I}\mathbin{|}\overline{Q})\to(s)(s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}][\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}]\mathsf{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}m}_{k}.P_{k}\mathbin{|}\overline{Q})=Q

. From

\Gamma_{i},\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i},n_{i},S_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,h
such that

•

\Gamma=\Gamma_{1},\dots,\Gamma_{h}

•

n=1+\sum_{i=1}^{h}n_{i}

•

\prod_{i=1}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}~\text{coherent}

•

\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}=\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{1}
and
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}=\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}
for some
i\in\{2,\dots,h\}

•

\Gamma_{1},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]:S_{1}\vdash^{n_{1}}s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}][\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}]\mathsf{!}\{\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}m}_{i}.P_{i}\}_{i\in I}

•

\Gamma_{i},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}]:S_{i}\vdash^{n_{i}}Q_{i}
for
i=2,\dots,h

From
[tm-tag]
we deduce that there exist
T_{i}
for all
i\in I
such that

•

S_{1}=\sum_{i\in I}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}m}_{i}.T_{i}

•

\Gamma_{1},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]:T_{i}\vdash^{n_{1}}P_{i}~{}^{(i\in I)}

From the hypothesis that
k\in I
we deduce that
\Gamma_{1},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]:T_{k}\vdash^{n_{1}}P_{k}
and from
[tm-tag]
we deduce

\Gamma_{1},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]:\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}m}_{k}.T_{k}\vdash^{n_{1}}s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}][\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}]\mathsf{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}m}_{k}.P_{k}
.
From
definition

2.3.2
we deduce that
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}m}_{k}.T_{k}\mathbin{|}\prod_{i=2}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}~\text{coherent}
.
We conclude with an application of
[tm-par]
taking
m\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny\sf def}}}{{=}}n
.

Case
[rm-tag]
.
Then
P=(s)(s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}][\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}]\mathsf{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}m}_{k}.P^{\prime}\mathbin{|}s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}][\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]\mathsf{?}\{\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}m}_{i}.Q_{i}\}_{i\in I}\mathbin{|}\overline{R})\to(s)(P^{\prime}\mathbin{|}Q_{k}\mathbin{|}\overline{R})=Q
and
k\in I
.
From
[tm-par]
we deduce that there exist
\Gamma_{i},S_{i},\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i},n_{i}

•

\Gamma=\Gamma_{1},\dots,\Gamma_{h}

•

\prod_{i=1}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}~\text{coherent}

•

\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}=\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{1}
and
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}=\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{2}

•

\Gamma_{1},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]:S_{1}\vdash^{n_{1}}s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}][\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}]\mathsf{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}m}_{k}.P^{\prime}

•

\Gamma_{2},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}]:S_{2}\vdash^{n_{2}}s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}][\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]\mathsf{?}\{\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}m}_{i}.Q_{i}\}_{i\in I}

•

\Gamma_{i},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}]:S_{i}\vdash^{n_{i}}R_{i}
for
i=3,\dots,h

From
[tm-tag]
we deduce that there exist
S^{\prime}_{1}
and
T_{i}
for every
i\in I
such that

•

S_{1}=\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}m}_{k}.S^{\prime}_{1}

•

\Gamma_{1},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]:S^{\prime}_{1}\vdash^{n_{1}}P^{\prime}

•

S_{2}=\sum_{i\in I}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}{?}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}m}_{i}.T_{i}

•

\Gamma_{2},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}]:T_{i}\vdash^{n_{2}}Q_{i}~{}^{(i\in I)}

From
definition

2.3.2
we deduce that
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}\triangleright{S^{\prime}_{1}}\mathbin{|}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}\triangleright{T_{k}}\mathbin{|}\prod_{i=2}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}~\text{coherent}
.
We conclude with an application of
[tm-par]
by taking
m\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny\sf def}}}{{=}}n
.

Case
[rm-par]
.
Then
P=(s)(P^{\prime}\mathbin{|}\overline{R})\to(s)(Q^{\prime}\mathbin{|}\overline{R})=Q
and
P^{\prime}\to Q^{\prime}
.
From
[tm-par]
we deduce that there exist
\Gamma_{i},\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i},S_{i},n_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,h
such that

•

\Gamma=\Gamma_{1},\dots,\Gamma_{h}

•

n=1+\sum_{i=1}^{h}n_{i}

•

\prod_{i=1}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}~\text{coherent}

•

\Gamma_{1},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{1}]:S_{1}\vdash^{n_{1}}P^{\prime}

•

\Gamma_{i},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}]:S_{i}\vdash^{n_{i}}R_{i}
for
i=2,\dots,h

Using the induction hypothesis on
\Gamma_{1},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{1}]:S_{1}\vdash^{n_{1}}P^{\prime}
and
P^{\prime}\to Q^{\prime}

we deduce
\Gamma_{1},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{1}]:S_{1}\vdash^{n^{\prime}_{1}}Q^{\prime}
for some
n^{\prime}_{1}
. We conclude with an application
of
[tm-par]
taking
m\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny\sf def}}}{{=}}1+n^{\prime}_{1}+\sum_{i=2}^{h}n_{i}
.

Case
[rm-cast]
.
Then
P=\lceil u\rceil{P^{\prime}}\to\lceil u\rceil{Q^{\prime}}=Q
and
P^{\prime}\to Q^{\prime}
.
From
[tm-cast]
we deduce that there exist
S,T,\Gamma^{\prime},n^{\prime},m_{u}
such that

•

\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime},u:S

•

S\leqslant_{m_{u}}T

•

n=m_{u}+n^{\prime}

•

\Gamma^{\prime},u:T\vdash^{n^{\prime}}P^{\prime}

Using the induction hypothesis on
\Gamma^{\prime},u:T\vdash^{n^{\prime}}P^{\prime}
and
P^{\prime}\to Q^{\prime}
we deduce
\Gamma^{\prime},u:T\vdash^{m^{\prime}}Q^{\prime}
for some
m^{\prime}
.
We conclude with an application of
[tm-cast]
taking
m\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny\sf def}}}{{=}}m_{u}+m^{\prime}
.

Case
[rm-struct]
.
Then
P\preccurlyeq P^{\prime}\to Q^{\prime}\preccurlyeq Q
.
From
Lemma

5.3.2
we deduce that
\Gamma\vdash^{n^{\prime}}P^{\prime}
for some
n^{\prime}\leq n
.
Using the induction hypothesis on
\Gamma\vdash^{n^{\prime}}P^{\prime}
and
P^{\prime}\to Q^{\prime}
we deduce
\Gamma\vdash^{m^{\prime}}Q^{\prime}
for some
m^{\prime}
.
We conclude using
lemma

5.3.2
once more.
∎

5.3.2
Measure

We introduce two fundamental notions for the soundness proof of the type system.
First, we introduce the
rank
of a session map
M
as the minimum length to reach
successful termination of session
M
.
Then, we introduce the
measure
of a process which takes into account the rank in the typing judgment
as well as the ranks of the session that have been already opened.
We embed such measure in the typing derivations by using a refined set of rules.
At last, we compare the typing judgments labeled with the usual rank with those including the measure (see
Lemma

5.3.4
)
and we prove that structural precongruence of processes does not increase the measure (see
Lemma

5.3.5
).

\frac{\mathstrut}{\Gamma\vDash^{(n,0)}P}\quad\textsc{mtm-thread}\Gamma\vdash^{n}P\hypertarget{rule:mtm-thread}{}\and\frac{\Gamma,u:T\vDash^{\mu}P}{\Gamma,u:S\vDash^{\mu+(n,0)}\lceil u\rceil P}\quad\textsc{mtm-cast}~S\leqslant_{n}T\hypertarget{rule:mtm-cast}{}\and\frac{\Gamma_{i},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}]:S_{i}\vDash^{\mu_{i}}P_{i}~{}^{(i=1,\dots,h)}}{\Gamma_{1},\dots,\Gamma_{h}\vDash^{\sum_{i=1}^{h}\mu_{i}+(0,\|\{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}\}_{i=1,\dots,h}\|)}(s)(P_{1}\mathbin{|}\dots\mathbin{|}P_{h})}\quad\textsc{mtm-par}~{\#}{\{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}\}_{i=1..h}}\hypertarget{rule:mtm-par}{}

Figure 5.5:
Typing rules with measure

Definition 5.3.1

(rank)
.

The
rank
of a session map
M=\prod_{i=1}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}
, written
\|M\|
, is the element of
\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
defined as

\|M\|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny\sf def}}}{{=}}\min|M\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{?}\checkmark}}{{\Longrightarrow}}|

where
|M\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha}}{{\Longrightarrow}}N|
denotes the length of the sequence
\tau,\dots,\tau,\alpha
and we postulate that
\min\emptyset=\infty
.

Definition 5.3.2

(Measure)
.

The measure of a process is a lexicographically ordered pair of natural numbers

(m,n)
where:

•

m
is an upper bound to the number of sessions that the process may open
and of weights of casts that the process may perform
in the future
before
it terminates;

•

n
is the overall effort for terminating the sessions that have been
already opened
in the past
,
i.e.
the sum of their rank (
Definition

5.3.1
).

In
Figure

5.5
we introduce a refined set of typing rules for processes that allow us to
associate them with their measure, not just with their rank.
The idea behind these rules (similarly to
Figure

4.5
) is that they distinguish between
past
and

future
of a process by looking at its structure. Indeed, unguarded
sessions have been created, casts have not been performed yet and sessions that
occur guarded have not been created yet.

[mtm-thread]
adopts the rank of the process inside the usual typing
judgment (
Figure

5.4
) as first component of the measure. This rule has lower
priority with respect to the other rules so that it is applied to processes that
are not casts or restrictions.
In
[mtm-cast]
the first component of the measure is increased by the
weight of the cast.

[mtm-par]
increases the second component of the measure by the rank of
the involved session.

Lemma 5.3.4
.

The following properties hold:

1.

\Gamma\vdash^{n}P
implies
\Gamma\vDash^{\mu}P
for some

\mu\leq(n,0)
;

2.

\Gamma\vDash^{\mu}P
implies
\Gamma\vdash^{n}P
for some
n
such that
\mu\leq(n,0)
.

Proof.

We prove item 1 by induction on the structure of
P
.
The proof of item 2 is by a straightforward induction over
\Gamma\vDash^{\mu}P
.

Case
P=(s)(\overline{P})
.
From
[tm-par]
we deduce that there exist
\Gamma_{i},\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i},S_{i},n_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,h
such that

•

\Gamma=\Gamma_{1},\dots,\Gamma_{h}

•

n=1+\sum_{i=1}^{h}n_{i}

•

\prod_{i=1}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}~\text{coherent}

•

\Gamma_{i},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}]:S_{i}\vdash^{n_{i}}P_{i}~{}^{(i=1,\dots,h)}

Using the induction hypothesis on
\Gamma_{i},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}]:S_{i}\vdash^{n_{i}}P_{i}~{}^{(i=1,\dots,h)}
we deduce
that there exist
\mu_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,h
such that

•

\Gamma_{i},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}]:S_{i}\vDash^{\mu_{i}}P_{i}~{}^{(i=1,\dots,h)}

•

\mu_{i}\leq(n_{i},0)
for
i=1,\dots,h

We conclude with one application of
[mtm-par]
by taking

\mu\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny\sf def}}}{{=}}\sum_{i=1}^{h}\mu_{i}+(0,\|\prod_{i=1}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}\|)
and observing that

\mu<(n_{1},0)+(n_{2},0)+\dots+(n_{h},0)+(1,0)=(n,0)
.

Case
P=\lceil u\rceil{Q}
.
From
[tm-cast]
we deduce that there exist
\Delta,S,T,m
and
m_{u}
such that

•

\Gamma=\Delta,u:S

•

S\leqslant_{m_{u}}T

•

n=m_{u}+m

•

\Delta,u:T\vdash^{m}Q

Using the induction hypothesis on
\Delta,u:T\vdash^{m}Q
we deduce
\Delta,u:T\vDash^{\nu}Q
for some
\nu\leq(m,0)
.
We conclude with an application of
[mtm-cast]
by taking
\mu\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny\sf def}}}{{=}}\nu+(m_{u},0)

and observing that
\mu\leq(m,0)+(m_{u},0)=(n,0)
.

In all the other cases
. We conclude with an application of
[mtm-thread]
by taking
\mu\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny\sf def}}}{{=}}(n,0)
.
∎

Lemma 5.3.5
.

If
\Gamma\vDash^{\mu}P
and
P\preccurlyeq Q
, then there exists
\nu\leq\mu
such that
\Gamma\vDash^{\nu}Q
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation of
P\preccurlyeq Q
and by cases on the last rule applied. We only consider the base cases.

Case
[sm-par-comm]
.
Then
P=(s)(\overline{P}\mathbin{\mid}P^{\prime}\mathbin{\mid}Q^{\prime}\mathbin{\mid}\overline{Q})\preccurlyeq(s)(\overline{P}\mathbin{\mid}Q^{\prime}\mathbin{\mid}P^{\prime}\mathbin{\mid}\overline{Q})=Q
.
From rule
[mtm-par]
we deduce that there exist
\Gamma_{i},\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i},S_{i},\mu_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,h
such that

•

\Gamma=\Gamma_{1},\dots,\Gamma_{h}

•

\mu=\sum_{i=1}^{h}\mu_{i}+(0,\|\prod_{i=1}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}\|)

•

\prod_{i=1}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}~\text{coherent}

•

\Gamma_{i},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}]:S_{i}\vDash^{\mu_{i}}P_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,k

•

\Gamma_{k+1},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{k+1}]:S_{k+1}\vDash^{\mu_{k+1}}P^{\prime}

•

\Gamma_{k+2},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{k+2}]:S_{k+2}\vDash^{\mu_{k+2}}Q^{\prime}

•

\Gamma_{i},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}]:S_{i}\vDash^{\mu_{i}}Q_{i}
for
i=k+3,\dots,h

We conclude
\Gamma\vDash^{\nu}Q
with one application of
[mtm-par]
by taking
\nu\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny\sf def}}}{{=}}\mu
.

Case
[sm-par-assoc]
.
Then
P=(s)(\overline{P}\mathbin{\mid}(t)(R\mathbin{\mid}\overline{Q}))\preccurlyeq(t)((s)(\overline{P}\mathbin{\mid}R)\mathbin{\mid}\overline{Q})=Q
and
s\in\mathsf{fn}(R)
.
From rule
[mtm-par]
we deduce that there exist
\Gamma_{i},\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i},S_{i},\mu_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,h
such that

•

\Gamma=\Gamma_{1},\dots,\Gamma_{h}

•

\mu=\sum_{i=1}^{h}\mu_{i}+(0,\|\prod_{i=1}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}\|)

•

\prod_{i=1}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}~\text{coherent}

•

\Gamma_{i},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}]:S_{i}\vDash^{\mu_{i}}P_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,h-1

•

\Gamma_{h},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{h}]:S_{h}\vDash^{\mu_{h}}(t)(R\mathbin{\mid}\overline{Q})

From rule
[mtm-par]
and the hypothesis that
s\in\mathsf{fn}(R)
we deduce that there exist

\Delta_{i},\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}_{i},T_{i},\nu_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,k
such that

•

\Gamma_{h}=\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{k}

•

\mu_{h}=\sum_{1}^{k}\nu_{i}+(0,\|\prod_{i=1}^{k}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}_{i}\triangleright{T_{i}}\|)

•

\prod_{i=1}^{k}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}_{i}\triangleright{T_{i}}~\text{coherent}

•

\Delta_{1},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{h}]:S_{h},t[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}_{1}]:T_{1}\vDash^{\nu_{1}}R

•

\Delta_{i+1},t[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}_{i+1}]:T_{i+1}\vDash^{\nu_{i+1}}Q_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,k-1

Using
[tm-par]
we deduce

•

\Gamma_{1},\dots,\Gamma_{h-1},\Delta_{1},t[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}_{1}]:T_{1}\vDash^{\sum_{i=1}^{h-1}{\mu_{i}}+\nu_{1}+\|\prod_{i=1}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}\|}(s)(\overline{P}\mathbin{\mid}R)

We conclude
\Gamma\vDash^{\nu}(t)((s)(\overline{P}\mathbin{\mid}R)\mathbin{\mid}\overline{Q})
with another
application of
[mtm-par]
by taking
\nu\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny\sf def}}}{{=}}\mu
.

Case
[sm-cast-comm]
.
Then
P=\lceil u\rceil{\lceil v\rceil{R}}\preccurlyeq\lceil v\rceil{\lceil u\rceil{R}}=Q
. We can assume
u\neq v
or else
P=Q
.
From rule
[mtm-cast]
we deduce that there exist
\Gamma_{1},S,T,\mu_{1},m_{u}
such that

•

\Gamma=\Gamma_{1},u:S

•

S\leqslant_{m_{u}}T

•

\mu=\mu_{1}+(m_{u},0)

•

\Gamma_{1},u:T\vDash^{\mu_{1}}\lceil v\rceil{R}

From rule
[tm-cast]
we deduce that there exist
\Gamma_{2},S^{\prime},T^{\prime},\mu_{2},m_{v}
such that

•

\Gamma_{1}=\Gamma_{2},v:S^{\prime}

•

S^{\prime}\leqslant_{m_{v}}T^{\prime}

•

\mu_{1}=\mu_{2}+(m_{v},0)

•

\Gamma_{2},u:T,v:T^{\prime}\vDash^{\mu_{2}}R

We derive
\Gamma_{2},u:S,v:T^{\prime}\vDash^{\mu_{2}+(m_{u},0)}\lceil u\rceil{R}
with one application of
[mtm-cast]

and we conclude with another application of
[mtm-cast]
by taking
\nu\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny\sf def}}}{{=}}\mu
.

Case
[sm-cast-new]
.
Then
P=(s)(\lceil s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]\rceil{R}\mathbin{\mid}\overline{P})\preccurlyeq(s)(R\mathbin{\mid}\overline{P})=Q
.
From rule
[mtm-par]
we deduce that there exist
\Delta,\mu^{\prime},S
and
\Gamma_{i},\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}_{i},S_{i},\mu_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,h
such that

•

\Gamma=\Delta,\Gamma_{1},\dots,\Gamma_{h}

•

\mu=\mu^{\prime}+\sum_{i=1}^{h}\mu_{i}+(0,\|\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}\triangleright{S}\mathbin{\mid}\prod_{i=1}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}\|)

•

\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}\triangleright{S}\mathbin{|}\prod_{i=1}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}~\text{coherent}

•

\Delta,s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]:S\vDash^{\mu^{\prime}}\lceil s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]\rceil{R}

•

\Gamma_{i},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}_{i}]:S_{i}\vDash^{\mu_{i}}P_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,h

From rule
[mtm-cast]
we deduce that there exist
T,\nu^{\prime},m_{s}
such that

•

S\leqslant_{m_{s}}T

•

\nu^{\prime}=\mu^{\prime}+(m_{s},0)

•

\Delta,s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]:T\vDash^{\nu^{\prime}}R

From
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}\triangleright{S}\mathbin{\mid}\prod_{i=1}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}~\text{coherent}
,
S\leqslant_{m_{s}}T
and
definition

3.2.1

we deduce
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}\triangleright{T}\mathbin{\mid}\prod_{i=1}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}~\text{coherent}
.
We conclude with an application of
[mtm-par]
by taking

\nu=\nu^{\prime}+\sum_{i=1}^{h}\mu_{i}+(0,\|\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}\triangleright{S}\mathbin{\mid}\prod_{i=1}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}\|)\leq n
.

Case
[sm-cast-swap]
.
Then
P=(s)(\lceil t[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]\rceil{R}\mathbin{\mid}\overline{P})\preccurlyeq\lceil t[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]\rceil{(s)(R\mathbin{\mid}\overline{P})}=Q
and
t\neq s
.
From rule
[mtm-par]
we deduce that there exist
\Gamma_{i},\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}_{i},\mu_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,h
such that

•

\Gamma=\Gamma_{1},\dots,\Gamma_{h}

•

\mu=\sum_{i=1}^{h}\mu_{i}+(0,\|\prod_{i=1}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}\|)

•

\prod_{i=1}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}~\text{coherent}

•

\Gamma_{1},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}_{1}]:S_{1}\vDash^{\mu_{1}}\lceil t[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]\rceil{R}

•

\Gamma_{i},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}_{i}]:S_{i}\vDash^{\mu_{i}}P_{i}
for
i=2,\dots,h

From rule
[mtm-cast]
we deduce that there exist
\Delta,T,\mu^{\prime},m_{t}
such that

•

\Gamma_{1}=\Delta,t[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]:S

•

S\leqslant_{m_{t}}T

•

\mu_{1}=\mu^{\prime}+(m_{t},0)

•

\Delta,t[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]:T,s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}_{1}]:S_{1}\vDash^{\mu^{\prime}}R

We derive

\Delta,t[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]:T,\Gamma_{2},\dots,\Gamma_{h}\vDash^{\mu^{\prime}+\sum_{i=2}^{h}\mu_{i}+(0,\|\prod_{i=1}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}q}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}\|)}(s)(R\mathbin{\mid}\overline{P})

with an application of
[mtm-par]
. We conclude with an application of
[mtm-cast]
by taking
m\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny\sf def}}}{{=}}n
.

Case
[sm-call]
.
Then
P=A\langle\overline{u}\rangle\preccurlyeq R\{\overline{x}/\overline{u}\}=Q
and
A(\overline{x})\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\vartriangle}}{{=}}}}R
.
From
[mtm-thread]
we deduce that
\Gamma\vDash^{n}A\langle\overline{u}\rangle
for some
n
such that
\mu=(n,0)
.
Using
Lemma

5.3.2
we deduce
\Gamma\vdash^{m}Q
for some
m\leq n
.
Using
Lemma

5.3.4
we deduce that
\Gamma\vDash^{\nu}Q
for some
\nu\leq(m,0)
.
We conclude observing that
\nu\leq(m,0)\leq(n,0)=\mu
.
∎

5.3.3
Normal Forms

As in
Section

4.3.3
we need to prove that a well typed process is deadlock free. The key lemma of this is
Lemma

5.3.11

that we dub
quasi deadlock freedom
. Informally, we want to rearrange the process under analysis
in order to put together the creation of some session
s
with all the subprocesses that start with
some communication on
s
. This way we can try to apply a reduction rule (see
Figure

5.3
).
This is what
Lemma

5.3.11
does.
However, a process organized as described is not guaranteed to make progress.
Indeed, for example all the subprocesses can be waiting for a message leading to a stuck process.
Hence, we called the lemma
quasi
deadlock freedom.
Deadlock freedom is achieved by taking into account the
coherence
of the session as well.
We called the shape of the process mentioned before
proximity normal form
(
Definition

5.3.5
).
In order to obtain a process in such form from a well typed one we require several steps.
We introduce additional normal forms (
Definitions

5.3.3
and
5.3.4
) to describe those processes in the middle of the procedure.
We introduce
process contexts
to easily refer to unguarded sub-processes:

\textbf{Process context}\quad\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D}~~::=~~[~]\mid(s)(\overline{P}\mathbin{|}\mathcal{C}\mathbin{|}\overline{Q})\mid\lceil u\rceil\mathcal{C}

Definition 5.3.3

(Choice Normal Form)
.

We say that
P_{1}\oplus P_{2}
is an
unguarded choice
of
P
if there
exists
\mathcal{C}
such that
P\preccurlyeq\mathcal{C}[P_{1}\oplus P_{2}]
. We say that

P
is in
choice normal form
if it has no unguarded choices.

Definition 5.3.4

(Thread Normal Form)
.

A process is in
thread normal form
if it is generated by the grammar below:

\begin{array}[]{@{}rcl@{}}P^{\mathit{nf}},Q^{\mathit{nf}}&::=&\lceil u\rceil P^{\mathit{nf}}\mid P^{\mathit{par}}\\
P^{\mathit{par}},Q^{\mathit{par}}&::=&(s)(\overline{P^{\mathit{par}}})\mid P^{\mathit{th}}\\
P^{\mathit{th}}&::=&\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}done}\mid\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}close}\,u\mid\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}wait}\,u.{P}\mid u[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]\pi\{\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}a}_{i}.P_{i}\}_{i\in I}\\
&&\mid u[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]\mathsf{!}v.P\mid u[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]\mathsf{?}(x).P\end{array}

Intuitively, a process is in
thread normal form
if it consists of an initial prefix of casts followed
by a parallel composition of threads, where a thread is either
done
or a process waiting to perform an input/output
action on some channel
u=s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]
for some
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}
. In this latter case, we say that the thread is an
s
-thread.

Definition 5.3.5

(Proximity Normal Form)
.

We say that
P^{\mathit{nf}}
is in
proximity normal form
if
P^{\mathit{nf}}=\mathcal{C}[(s)(\overline{P^{\mathit{th}}})]
for some
\mathcal{C}
,
s
,
\overline{P^{\mathit{th}}}
where
each
P^{\mathit{th}}_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,h
is a
s
-thread.

Lemma 5.3.6
.

If
\Gamma\vdash^{n}P
and
\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{ind}}P
, then there exists
Q
in choice
normal form such that
P\Rightarrow Q
and
\Gamma\vdash^{m}Q
for some
m\leq n
.

Proof.

By induction on
\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{ind}}P
and by cases on the last rule applied.

Case
P
is already in choice normal form
.
We conclude taking
Q\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny\sf def}}}{{=}}P
and
m\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny\sf def}}}{{=}}n
.

Case
[tm-call]
.
Then
P=A\langle\overline{u}\rangle
and
A(\overline{x})\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\vartriangle}}{{=}}}}R
.
We deduce
\Gamma=\overline{u:S}
,
A:[\overline{S};n^{\prime}]
and
\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{ind}}R\{\overline{u}/\overline{x}\}
. Moreover, it must be the case that

\Gamma\vdash^{n^{\prime}}R\{\overline{u}/\overline{x}\}
and
n^{\prime}\leq n
since
[tm-call]
is used in the coinductive judgment as well.
Using the induction hypothesis we deduce that there exist
Q
in choice normal form and
m\leq n^{\prime}
such that
R\{\overline{u}/\overline{x}\}\Rightarrow Q

and
\Gamma\vdash^{m}Q
.
We conclude by observing that
P\Rightarrow Q
using
[rm-struct]
and that
m\leq n^{\prime}\leq n
.

Case
[com-choice]
.
Then
P=P_{1}\oplus P_{2}
.
We deduce
\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{ind}}P_{k}
with
k\in\{1,2\}
.
Moreover, it must be the case that
\Gamma\vdash^{n}P_{k}
since
[tm-choice]
is used in the coinductive judgment.
Using the induction hypothesis we deduce that there exist
Q
in choice normal form and
m\leq n
such that
P_{k}\Rightarrow Q
and
\Gamma\vdash^{m}Q
.
We conclude by observing that
P\to P_{k}
by
[rm-choice]
.

Case
[tm-choice]
.
Analogous to the previous case but we consider the premise in which the rank is the same of the conclusion to keep sure that it does not increase.

Case
[tm-par]
.
Then
P=(s)(P_{1}\mathbin{|}\dots\mathbin{|}P_{h})
.
We deduce

•

\Gamma=\Gamma_{1},\dots,\Gamma_{h}

•

\Gamma_{i},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}]:S_{i}\vdash_{\mathsf{ind}}P_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,h

•

\prod_{i=1}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}~\text{coherent}

Furthermore, it must be the case that
\Gamma_{i},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}]:S_{i}\vdash^{n_{i}}P_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,h

and
n=1+\sum_{i=1}^{h}n_{i}
since
[tm-par]
is used in the coinductive judgment as well.
Using the induction hypothesis we deduce that there exist
Q_{i}
in choice normal form and
m_{i}\leq n_{i}
such that

P_{i}\Rightarrow Q_{i}
and
\Gamma_{i},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}]:S_{i}\vdash^{m_{i}}Q_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,h
.
We conclude by taking
m\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny\sf def}}}{{=}}1+\sum_{i=1}^{h}m_{i}
and
Q\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny\sf def}}}{{=}}(s)(Q_{1}\mathbin{|}\cdots\mathbin{|}Q_{h})

with one application of
[tm-par]
, observing that

m=1+\sum_{i=1}^{h}m_{i}\leq 1+\sum_{i=1}^{h}n_{i}=n
and that
P\Rightarrow Q
by
[rm-par]
.

Case
[tm-cast]
.
Then
P=\lceil u\rceil P^{\prime}
.
Analogous to the previous case, just simpler.
∎

Lemma 5.3.7
.

If
\Gamma\vdash^{n}P
, then there exists
Q
in choice normal form such that
P\Rightarrow Q
and
\Gamma\vdash^{m}Q
for some
m\leq n
.

Proof.

Consequence of
Lemma

5.3.6
noting that
\Gamma\vdash^{n}P
implies
\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{ind}}P
.
∎

Lemma 5.3.8
.

If
\Gamma\vDash^{\mu}P
, then there exist
Q
in choice normal form and
\nu\leq\mu
such that
P\Rightarrow Q
and
\Gamma\vDash^{\nu}Q
.

Proof.

By induction on
\Gamma\vDash^{\mu}P
and by cases on the last rule applied.

Case
[mtm-thread]
.
Then
P
is a thread. We deduce that

•

\mu=(n,0)
for some
n

•

\Gamma\vdash^{n}P

From
Lemma

5.3.7
we deduce that there exist
Q
and
m\leq n
such that
P\Rightarrow Q
and
\Gamma\vdash^{m}Q
.
From
Lemma

5.3.4
we deduce
\Gamma\vDash^{\nu}Q
for some
\nu\leq(m,0)
.
We conclude observing that
\nu\leq(m,0)\leq(n,0)=\mu
.

Case
[mtm-cast]
.
Then
P=\lceil u\rceil{P^{\prime}}
. We deduce that

•

\Gamma=\Delta,u:S

•

S\leqslant_{n}T

•

\mu=\mu^{\prime}+(n,0)

•

\Gamma^{\prime},u:T\vDash^{\mu^{\prime}}P^{\prime}

Using the induction hypothesis we deduce that there exist
Q^{\prime}
and
\nu^{\prime}\leq\mu^{\prime}
such that
P^{\prime}\Rightarrow Q^{\prime}

and
\Gamma^{\prime},u:T\vDash^{\nu^{\prime}}Q^{\prime}
. We conclude with an application of
[mtm-cast]
taking

Q\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny\sf def}}}{{=}}\lceil u\rceil{Q^{\prime}}
,
\nu\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny\sf def}}}{{=}}\nu^{\prime}+(n,0)
and observing that
P\Rightarrow Q
using
[rm-cast]
.

Case
[mtm-par]
.
Then
P=(s)(P_{1}\mathbin{|}\dots\mathbin{|}P_{h})
.
We deduce

•

\Gamma=\Gamma_{1},\dots,\Gamma_{h}

•

\mu=\sum_{i=1}^{h}\mu_{i}+(0,\|\prod_{i=1}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}\|)

•

\Gamma_{i},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}]:S_{i}\vDash^{\mu_{i}}P_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,h

•

\prod_{i=1}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}~\text{coherent}

Using the induction hypothesis we deduce that there exist
Q_{i}
in choice normal form and
\nu_{i}\leq\mu_{i}
such that
P_{i}\Rightarrow Q_{i}
and

\Gamma_{i},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}]:S_{i}\vDash^{\nu_{i}}Q_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,h
.
We conclude by taking
\nu\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny\sf def}}}{{=}}\sum_{i=1}^{h}\nu_{i}+(0,\|\prod_{i=1}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}\|)
and

Q\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny\sf def}}}{{=}}(s)(Q_{1}\mathbin{|}\cdots\mathbin{|}Q_{h})
with one application of
[mtm-par]
,
observing that

\nu=\sum_{i=1}^{h}\nu_{i}+(0,\|\prod_{i=1}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}\|)\leq\sum_{i=1}^{h}\mu_{i}+(0,\|\prod_{i=1}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}\|)=\mu

and that
P\Rightarrow Q
by
[rm-par]
.
∎

Lemma 5.3.9
.

If
\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{ind}}P
and
P
is in choice normal form, then there exists
P^{\mathit{nf}}
such that
P\preccurlyeq P^{\mathit{nf}}
.

Proof.

By induction on
\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{ind}}P
and by cases on the last rule applied.

Cases
[tm-choice]
and
[com-choice]
. These cases are impossible from the hypothesis that
P
is in choice normal form.

Cases
[tm-done]
,
[tm-wait]
and
[tm-close]
.
Then
P
is a thread and is already in thread normal form and we conclude by reflexivity of
\preccurlyeq
.

[tm-channel-in]
,
[tm-channel-out]
,
[tm-tag]
and
[com-tag]
.
Then
P
is a thread and is already in thread normal form and we conclude by reflexivity of
\preccurlyeq
.

Case
[tm-call]
.
Then there exist
A
,
Q
,
\overline{u}
and
\overline{S}
such that

•

P=A\langle\overline{u}\rangle

•

A(\overline{x})\mathrel{\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\vartriangle}}{{=}}}}Q

•

\Gamma=\overline{u:S}

•

\overline{u:S}\vdash_{\mathsf{ind}}Q\{\overline{u}/\overline{x}\}

Using the induction hypothesis on
\overline{u:S}\vdash_{\mathsf{ind}}Q\{\overline{u}/\overline{x}\}
we deduce that there exists

P^{\mathit{nf}}
such that
Q\{\overline{u}/\overline{x}\}\preccurlyeq P^{\mathit{nf}}
.
We conclude
P\preccurlyeq P^{\mathit{nf}}
using
[sm-call]
and the transitivity of
\preccurlyeq
.

Case
[tm-par]
.
Then there exist
s
and
P_{i},\Gamma_{i},S_{i},\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,h
such that

•

P=(s)(P_{1}\mathbin{|}\cdots\mathbin{|}P_{h})

•

\Gamma=\Gamma_{1},\dots,\Gamma_{h}

•

\Gamma_{i},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}]:S_{i}\vdash_{\mathsf{ind}}P_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,h

Using the induction hypothesis on
\Gamma_{i},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}]:S_{i}\vdash_{\mathsf{ind}}P_{i}
we deduce that there exist

P^{\mathit{nf}}_{i}
such that
P_{i}\preccurlyeq P^{\mathit{nf}}_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,h
.
By definition of thread normal form, it must be the case that
P^{\mathit{nf}}_{i}=\lceil\overline{u_{i}}\rceil P^{\mathit{par}}_{i}

for some
\overline{u_{i}}
and
P^{\mathit{par}}_{i}
. Let
\overline{v_{i}}
be the same sequence as
\overline{u_{i}}

except that occurrences of
s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}]
have been removed.
We conclude by taking
P^{\mathit{nf}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny\sf def}}}{{=}}\lceil\overline{v_{1}}\dots\overline{v_{h}}\rceil(s)(P^{\mathit{par}}_{1}\mathbin{|}\dots\mathbin{|}P^{\mathit{par}}_{h})

and using the fact that
\preccurlyeq
is a pre-congruence and observing that

\begin{array}[]{rcll}P&=&(s)(P_{1}\mathbin{|}\cdots\mathbin{|}P_{h})&\text{by definition of $P$}\\
&\preccurlyeq&(s)(P^{\mathit{nf}}_{1}\mathbin{|}\cdots\mathbin{|}P^{\mathit{nf}}_{h})&\text{using the induction hypothesis}\\
&=&(s)(\lceil\overline{u_{1}}\rceil P^{\mathit{par}}_{1}\mathbin{|}\cdots\mathbin{|}\lceil\overline{u_{h}}\rceil P^{\mathit{par}}_{h})&\text{by \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{def:tnf_multi}}\\
&\preccurlyeq&\lceil\overline{v_{1}}\dots\overline{v_{h}}\rceil(s)(P^{\mathit{par}}_{1}\mathbin{|}\cdots\mathbin{|}P^{\mathit{par}}_{h})&\text{by \hyperlink{rule:sm-cast-new}{{\small[sm-cast-new]}},}\\
&&&\text{\hyperlink{rule:sm-cast-swap}{{\small[sm-cast-swap]}}, \hyperlink{rule:sm-par-comm}{{\small[sm-par-comm]}}}\\
&=&P^{\mathit{nf}}&\text{by definition of $P^{\mathit{nf}}$}\end{array}

Case
[tm-cast]
.
Then there exist
u
,
Q
,
\Gamma^{\prime}
,
S
and
T
such that

•

P=\lceil u\rceil Q

•

\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime},u:S

•

\Gamma^{\prime},u:T\vdash_{\mathsf{ind}}Q

•

S\leqslant T

Using the induction hypothesis on
\Gamma^{\prime},u:T\vdash_{\mathsf{ind}}Q
we deduce that there exists
Q^{\mathit{nf}}
such that
Q\preccurlyeq Q^{\mathit{nf}}
.
We conclude by taking
P^{\mathit{nf}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny\sf def}}}{{=}}\lceil u\rceil Q^{\mathit{nf}}
using the fact that
\preccurlyeq
is a pre-congruence.
∎

Lemma 5.3.10

(Proximity)
.

If
s\in\mathsf{fn}(P)\setminus\mathsf{bn}(\mathcal{C})
, then
(s)(\mathcal{C}[P]\mathbin{|}\overline{Q})\preccurlyeq\mathcal{D}[(s)(P\mathbin{|}\overline{Q})]
for some
\mathcal{D}
.

Proof.

By induction on the structure of
\mathcal{C}
and by cases on its shape.

Case
\mathcal{C}=[~]
.
We conclude by taking
\mathcal{D}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny\sf def}}}{{=}}[~]
using the reflexivity of
\preccurlyeq
.

Case
\mathcal{C}=(t)(\overline{P^{\prime}}\mathbin{|}\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\mathbin{|}\overline{Q^{\prime}})
.
From the hypothesis
s\in\mathsf{fn}(P)\setminus\mathsf{bn}(\mathcal{C})
we deduce
s\neq t
and
s\in\mathsf{fn}(P)\setminus\mathsf{bn}(\mathcal{C}^{\prime})
.
Using the induction hypothesis and
[sm-par-comm]
we deduce that there exists
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
such that

(s)(\mathcal{C}^{\prime}[P]\mathbin{|}\overline{Q})\preccurlyeq\mathcal{D}^{\prime}[(s)(P\mathbin{|}\overline{Q})]
.
Take
\mathcal{D}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny\sf def}}}{{=}}(t)(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\mathbin{|}\overline{P^{\prime}}\mathbin{|}\overline{Q^{\prime}})
. We conclude

\begin{array}[]{rcll}(s)(\mathcal{C}[P]\mathbin{|}\overline{Q})&=&(s)((t)(\overline{P^{\prime}}\mathbin{|}\mathcal{C}^{\prime}[P]\mathbin{|}\overline{Q^{\prime}})\mathbin{|}\overline{Q})&\text{by definition of $\mathcal{C}$}\\
&\preccurlyeq&(s)(\overline{Q}\mathbin{|}(t)(\mathcal{C}^{\prime}[P]\mathbin{|}\overline{P^{\prime}}\mathbin{|}\overline{Q^{\prime}}))&\text{by \hyperlink{rule:sm-par-comm}{{\small[sm-par-comm]}}}\\
&\preccurlyeq&(t)((s)(\overline{Q}\mathbin{|}\mathcal{C}^{\prime}[P])\mathbin{|}\overline{P^{\prime}}\mathbin{|}\overline{Q^{\prime}})&\text{by \hyperlink{rule:sm-par-assoc}{{\small[sm-par-assoc]}}}\\
&&&\text{and $s\in\mathsf{fn}(\mathcal{C}^{\prime}[P])$}\\
&\preccurlyeq&(t)((s)(\mathcal{C}^{\prime}[P]\mathbin{|}\overline{Q})\mathbin{|}\overline{P^{\prime}}\mathbin{|}\overline{Q^{\prime}})&\text{by \hyperlink{rule:sm-par-comm}{{\small[sm-par-comm]}}}\\
&\preccurlyeq&(t)(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}[(s)(P\mathbin{|}\overline{Q})]\mathbin{|}\overline{P^{\prime}}\mathbin{|}\overline{Q^{\prime}})&\text{by induction hypothesis}\\
&=&\mathcal{D}[(s)(P\mathbin{|}\overline{Q})]&\text{by definition of $\mathcal{D}$}\end{array}

Case
\mathcal{C}=\lceil t[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]\rceil\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
and
s\neq t
.
Using the induction hypothesis we deduce that there exists
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
such that

(s)(\mathcal{C}^{\prime}[P]\mathbin{|}\overline{Q})\preccurlyeq\mathcal{D}^{\prime}[(s)(P\mathbin{|}\overline{Q})]
.
Take
\mathcal{D}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny\sf def}}}{{=}}\lceil t[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]\rceil\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
. We conclude

\begin{array}[]{rcll}(s)(\mathcal{C}[P]\mathbin{|}\overline{Q})&=&(s)(\lceil t[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]\rceil\mathcal{C}^{\prime}[P]\mathbin{|}\overline{Q})&\text{by definition of $\mathcal{C}$}\\
&\preccurlyeq&\lceil t[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]\rceil(s)(\mathcal{C}^{\prime}[P]\mathbin{|}\overline{Q})&\text{by \hyperlink{rule:sm-cast-swap}{{\small[sm-cast-swap]}} and $t\neq s$}\\
&\preccurlyeq&\lceil t[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]\rceil\mathcal{D}^{\prime}[(s)(P\mathbin{|}\overline{Q})]&\text{using the induction hypothesis}\\
&=&\mathcal{D}[(s)(P\mathbin{|}\overline{Q})]&\text{by definition of $\mathcal{D}$}\end{array}

Case
\mathcal{C}=\lceil s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]\rceil\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
.
Using the induction hypothesis we deduce that there exists
\mathcal{D}
such that

(s)(\mathcal{C}^{\prime}[P]\mathbin{|}\overline{Q})\preccurlyeq\mathcal{D}[(s)(P\mathbin{|}\overline{Q})]
.
We conclude

\begin{array}[b]{rcll}(s)(\mathcal{C}[P]\mathbin{|}\overline{Q})&=&(s)(\lceil s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}]\rceil\mathcal{C}^{\prime}[P]\mathbin{|}\overline{Q})&\text{by definition of $\mathcal{C}$}\\
&\preccurlyeq&(s)(\mathcal{C}^{\prime}[P]\mathbin{|}\overline{Q})&\text{by \hyperlink{rule:sm-cast-new}{{\small[sm-cast-new]}}}\\
&\preccurlyeq&\mathcal{D}[(s)(P\mathbin{|}\overline{Q})]&\text{using the induction hypothesis}\end{array}\qed

Lemma 5.3.11

(Quasi - Deadlock Freedom)
.

If
\emptyset\vDash^{\mu}P^{\mathit{nf}}
, then
P^{\mathit{nf}}=\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}done}
or
P^{\mathit{nf}}\preccurlyeq Q^{\mathit{nf}}
for some
Q^{\mathit{nf}}
in proximity normal form.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation of
\emptyset\vDash^{\mu}P^{\mathit{nf}}
we deduce that

P^{\mathit{nf}}
consists of
s_{1},\dots,s_{h}
sessions and
\sum_{i=1}^{h}k_{i}-h+1
threads where

k_{i}
is the number of roles in
s_{i}
. The scenarios in which no communication is possible are those
in which for each session
s_{i}
there are less than
k_{i}

s_{i}
-threads. If we assume that
for each
s_{i}
there are
k_{i}-1
threads, then we obtain

\sum_{i=1}^{h}k_{i}-h+1-\sum_{i=1}^{h}(k_{i}-1)=\sum_{i=1}^{h}k_{i}-h+1-\sum_{i=1}^{h}k_{i}+h=1

s_{i}
-thread for some
s_{i}
; hence, there exist
k_{i}

s_{i}
-threads.
In other words, there exist
\mathcal{D},\mathcal{C}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{C}_{k_{i}}
and
P^{\mathit{th}}_{1},\dots,P^{\mathit{th}}_{k_{i}}

s_{i}
-threads
such that

P^{\mathit{nf}}=\mathcal{D}[(s_{i})(\mathcal{C}_{1}[P^{\mathit{th}}_{1}]\mathbin{|}\cdots\mathbin{|}\mathcal{C}_{k_{i}}[P^{\mathit{th}}_{k_{i}}])]

We conclude

\begin{array}[]{rcl}P^{\mathit{nf}}&=&\mathcal{D}[(s_{i})(\mathcal{C}_{1}[P^{\mathit{th}}_{1}]\mathbin{|}\cdots\mathbin{|}\mathcal{C}_{k_{i}}[P^{\mathit{th}}_{k_{i}}])]\\
&&\hfill\text{by definition of $P^{\mathit{nf}}$}\\
&\preccurlyeq&\mathcal{D}[\mathcal{D}_{1}[(s_{i})(P^{\mathit{th}}_{1}\mathbin{|}\mathcal{C}_{2}[P^{\mathit{th}}_{2}]\mathbin{|}\cdots\mathbin{|}\mathcal{C}_{k_{i}}[P^{\mathit{th}}_{k_{i}}])]]\\
&&\hfill\text{by \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:proximity_multi}}\\
&\preccurlyeq&\mathcal{D}[\mathcal{D}_{1}[(s_{i})(\mathcal{C}_{2}[P^{\mathit{th}}_{2}]\mathbin{|}P^{\mathit{th}}_{1}\mathbin{|}\cdots\mathbin{|}\mathcal{C}_{k_{i}}[P^{\mathit{th}}_{k_{i}}])]]\\
&&\hfill\text{by \hyperlink{rule:sm-par-comm}{{\small[sm-par-comm]}}}\\
&\dots\\
&\preccurlyeq&\mathcal{D}[\mathcal{D}_{1}[\mathcal{D}_{2}[\dots\mathcal{D}_{k_{i}}[(s_{i})(P^{\mathit{th}}_{k_{i}}\mathbin{|}\cdots\mathbin{|}P^{\mathit{th}}_{2}\mathbin{|}P^{\mathit{th}}_{1})]\dots]]]\\
&&\hfill\text{for some $\mathcal{D}_{2},\dots,\mathcal{D}_{k_{i}}$ by \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:proximity_multi}}\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny\sf def}}}{{=}}&Q^{\mathit{nf}}\end{array}

The fact that
Q^{\mathit{nf}}
is in thread normal form follows from the observation
that
P^{\mathit{nf}}
does not have unguarded casts (it is a closed process in thread
normal form) so the pre-congruence rules applied here and in

Lemma

5.3.10
do not move casts around. We conclude that
Q^{\mathit{nf}}
is in
proximity normal form by its shape.
∎

As mentioned at the beginning,

Lemma

5.3.11
is dubbed “quasi-deadlock freedom” because it does not
say that
Q^{\mathit{nf}}
reduces. Indeed, a process in proximity normal form is only

ready to communicate
thanks to its shape (see reduction rules). We can
prove that a well typed process of such kind actually reduces by observing that

[tm-par]
requires that the involved session is coherent. This result is
the key ingredient for proving
Lemma

5.3.12
.

5.3.4
Soundness

The next key result we prove is inspired by the
helpful direction
. Given a well typed process in
proximity normal form (see
Definition

5.3.5
), we prove that there exists a reduct that is well typed
with a
strictly smaller
measure (see
Lemma

5.3.12
).
Notably, this result implies deadlock freedom.
Then, it is easy to prove that a well typed process is either
done
or it can reach
done
in finitely
many steps by induction over the measure.

Lemma 5.3.12
.

If
\Gamma\vDash^{\mu}P^{\mathit{nf}}
where
P^{\mathit{nf}}
is in proximity normal form, then there exist
Q
and
\nu<\mu

such that
P^{\mathit{nf}}\Rightarrow^{+}Q
and
\Gamma\vDash^{\nu}Q
.

Proof.

From the hypothesis that
P^{\mathit{nf}}
is in proximity normal form we know that

P^{\mathit{nf}}=\mathcal{C}[(s)(P^{\mathit{th}}_{1}\mathbin{|}\cdots\mathbin{|}P^{\mathit{th}}_{h})]
for some
\mathcal{C}
,
s
and
P^{\mathit{th}}_{1},\dots,P^{\mathit{th}}_{h}

s
-threads.
We reason by induction on
\mathcal{C}
and by cases on its shape.

Case
\mathcal{C}=[~]
.
From
[mtm-thread]
and
[mtm-par]
we deduce that there exist
\Gamma_{i},S_{i},\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i},n_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,h
such that

•

\Gamma=\Gamma_{1},\dots,\Gamma_{h}

•

\prod_{i=1}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}~\text{coherent}

•

\mu=(\sum_{i=1}^{h}n_{i},\|\prod_{i=1}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}\|)

•

\Gamma_{i},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}]:S_{i}\vdash^{n_{i}}P^{\mathit{th}}_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,h

From the hypothesis that
\prod_{i=1}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}~\text{coherent}
we deduce
\prod_{i=1}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{?}\checkmark}}{{\Longrightarrow}}
.
We now reason on the rank of the session and on the shape of
S_{i}
.
For the sake of simplicity, we implicitly apply
[s-par-comm]
at process level.

If
\|\prod_{i=1}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}\|=1
, then
\prod_{i=1}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{?}\checkmark}}{{\longrightarrow}}
using
[lm-terminate]
.

•

Case
S_{1}={?}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}end}
and
S_{j}={!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}end}
for
j=2,\dots,h
.
Then

–

\Gamma_{j}=\emptyset
and
P^{\mathit{th}}_{j}=\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}close}\,s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{j}]
for
j=2,\dots,h

–

P^{\mathit{th}}_{1}=\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}wait}\,s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{1}].{Q}

–

\Gamma_{1}\vdash^{n_{1}}Q

From
Lemma

5.3.4
we deduce that
\Gamma_{1}\vDash^{\nu}Q
for some
\nu\leq(n_{1},0)
.
We conclude observing that
P^{\mathit{nf}}\to Q
by
[rm-signal]
and that

\nu\leq(n_{1},0)<(\sum_{i=1}^{h}n_{i},\|\prod_{i=1}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}\|)=\mu
.

If
\|\prod_{i=1}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}\|>1
, then
\prod_{i=1}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\tau}}{{\longrightarrow}}\dots\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{?}\checkmark}}{{\longrightarrow}}

first using
[lm-tau]
and
[lm-sync]
.
Observe that
[lm-pick]
is never used since we are considering the minimum reduction sequence;
a synchronization through
[lm-pick]
and
[lm-sync]
would lead to a longer reduction. T
hen
S_{1}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{1}\triangleright{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{2}{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}m}_{k}}}
and
S_{2}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{2}\triangleright{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{1}{?}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}m}_{k}}}
for some

\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}m}_{k}
or
S_{1}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{1}\triangleright{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{2}}{!}S}
and
S_{2}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{1}\triangleright{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{2}}{?}S}
.

•

Case
S_{1}=\sum_{i\in I}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{2}{!}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}m}_{i}.S^{\prime}_{i}
and
S_{2}=\sum_{j\in J}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{1}{?}\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}m}_{j}.T_{j}
with
k\in I
.
From the hypothesis that
\prod_{i=1}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}~\text{coherent}
we deduce
I\subseteq J
.
From
Definition

2.3.2
we deduce

\prod_{i=3}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}\mathbin{|}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{1}\triangleright{S^{\prime}_{k}}\mathbin{|}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{2}\triangleright{T_{k}}~\text{coherent}

and from
[tm-tag]
we deduce that

–

P^{\mathit{th}}_{1}=s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{1}][\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{2}]{!}\{\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}m}_{i}.P^{\prime}_{i}\}_{i\in I}

–

P^{\mathit{th}}_{2}=s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{2}][\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{1}]{?}\{\textsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}m}_{j}.Q_{j}\}_{j\in J}

–

\Gamma_{1},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{1}]:S^{\prime}_{i}\vdash^{n_{1}}P^{\prime}_{i}
for all
i\in I

–

\Gamma_{2},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{2}]:T_{j}\vdash^{n_{2}}Q_{j}
for all
j\in J

Let
Q\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny\sf def}}}{{=}}(s)(P^{\prime}_{k}\mathbin{|}Q_{k}\mathbin{|}P_{3}\mathbin{|}\dots\mathbin{|}P_{h})
and observe that
P^{\mathit{nf}}\Rightarrow^{+}Q
by
[rm-pick]
and
[rm-tag]
.
From
Lemma

5.3.4
we deduce that there exist
\mu_{1}\leq(n_{1},0),\mu_{2}\leq(n_{2},0)
such that

–

\Gamma_{1},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{1}]:S^{\prime}_{k}\vDash^{\mu_{1}}P^{\prime}_{k}

–

\Gamma_{2},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{2}]:T_{k}\vDash^{\mu_{2}}Q_{k}

Let

\nu\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny\sf def}}}{{=}}\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}+(\sum_{i=3}^{h}n_{i},\|\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{1}\triangleright{S^{\prime}_{k}}\mathbin{|}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{2}\triangleright{T_{k}}\mathbin{|}\prod_{i=3}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}\|)
.
We conclude with one application of
[mtm-par]
observing that

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\nu&=&\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}+\\
&&(\sum_{i=3}^{h}n_{i},\|\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{1}\triangleright{S^{\prime}_{k}}\mathbin{|}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{2}\triangleright{T_{k}}\mathbin{|}\prod_{i=3}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}\|)&\text{by def. of $\nu$}\\
&\leq&(\sum_{i=1}^{h}n_{i},\|\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{1}\triangleright{S^{\prime}_{k}}\mathbin{|}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{2}\triangleright{T_{k}}\mathbin{|}\prod_{i=3}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}\|)&\text{by \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:measure_rank_multi}}\\
&<&(\sum_{i=1}^{h}n_{i},\|\prod_{i=1}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}\|)&\text{before $\to$}\\
&=&\mu\end{array}

•

Case
S_{1}=\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{2}{!}{S}.T_{1}
and
S_{2}=\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{1}{?}{S}.T_{2}
.

From the hypothesis that
\prod_{i=1}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}~\text{coherent}
and
Definition

2.3.2
we deduce

\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{1}\triangleright{T_{1}}\mathbin{|}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{2}\triangleright{T_{2}}\mathbin{|}\prod_{i=3}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}~\text{coherent}
and from

[tm-channel-out]
and
[tm-channel-in]
we deduce that

•

P^{\mathit{th}}_{1}=s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{1}][\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{2}]\mathsf{!}u.P^{\prime}_{1}

•

P^{\mathit{th}}_{2}=s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{2}][\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{1}]\mathsf{?}(x).P^{\prime}_{2}

•

\Gamma_{1},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{1}]:T_{1}\vdash^{n_{1}}P^{\prime}_{1}

•

\Gamma_{2},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{2}]:T_{2},x:S\vdash^{n_{2}}P^{\prime}_{2}

Let
Q\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny\sf def}}}{{=}}(s)(P^{\prime}_{1}\mathbin{|}P^{\prime}_{2}\{u/x\}\mathbin{|}P^{\mathit{th}}_{3}\mathbin{|}\cdots\mathbin{|}P^{\mathit{th}}_{h})
and observe that
P^{\mathit{nf}}\to Q
by
[rm-channel]
.
Using
Lemma

5.3.1
we deduce
\Gamma_{2},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{2}]:T_{2},u:S\vdash^{n_{2}}P^{\prime}_{2}\{u/x\}
and
from
Lemma

5.3.4
we deduce that there exist
\mu_{1}\leq(n_{1},0),\mu_{2}\leq(n_{2},0)
such that

•

\Gamma_{1},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{1}]:T_{1}\vDash^{\mu_{1}}P^{\prime}_{1}

•

\Gamma_{2},s[\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{2}]:T_{2},u:S\vDash^{\mu_{2}}P^{\prime}_{2}\{u/x\}

Let
\nu\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\tiny\sf def}}}{{=}}\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}+(\sum_{i=3}^{h}n_{i},\|\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{1}\triangleright{T_{1}}\mathbin{|}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{2}\triangleright{T_{2}}\mathbin{|}\prod_{i=3}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}\|)
.
We conclude with one application of
[mtm-par]
observing that

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\nu&=&\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}+\\
&&(\sum_{i=3}^{h}n_{i},\|\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{1}\triangleright{T_{1}}\mathbin{|}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{2}\triangleright{T_{2}}\mathbin{|}\prod_{i=3}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}\|)&\text{by definition of $\nu$}\\
&\leq&(\sum_{i=1}^{h}n_{i},\|\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{1}\triangleright{T_{1}}\mathbin{|}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{2}\triangleright{T_{2}}\mathbin{|}\prod_{i=3}^{h}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p}_{i}\triangleright{S_{i}}\|)&\text{by \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:measure_rank_multi}}\\
